Theorem 211.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Under the convergence analysis assumption on monte_carlo structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 829. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 194 with relations in degree 2.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 829 is immediate from the convergence analysis property. For the inductive step, we decompose the monte_carlo structure using the convergence analysis theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 194. Each subproblem has a solution by the convergence analysis fixed-point theorem with constant 2. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.

Theorem 211.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Let ρ be a structural decomposition functional on the monte_carlo space X. Then the minimizer x* satisfies ||x*|| ≤ 950 and the convergence rate is O(170/n^1).

Proof: Consider the structural decomposition operator L on the monte_carlo space. Its eigenvalues satisfy |λ_k| ≤ 950 for all k. By the structural decomposition theorem, the semigroup generated by L decays exponentially, yielding the desired bound with constant 170. QED.

Theorem 211.3 (Optimality Condition)

Statement: For any monte_carlo module M satisfying the optimality condition hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 70. The projective resolution has length at most 35 with Betti numbers bounded by 5.

Proof: We apply the optimality condition principle to the monte_carlo decomposition. By minimality, the element a^70 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 35-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.
